# phebs — Port Plan (Sourcebot → Go)

**Shape:** single Go binary · zoekt as a library · supervised SurrealDB · clean-room TypeScript SPA

**Version:** v2.3 (2026-07-09).
**Changelog:** v2.2 (2026-07-07) resolved huma/branch-scope/queue-wakeup at Uber-scale
posture and added the fleet profile. v2.3 re-baselines the doc for the phebs repo:
license verdict + reference-only discipline, own config schema (supersedes v2.2
config row), naming, and the BACKLOG.md epic mapping.
**Provenance:** v2.2 baseline reconstructed 2026-07-09 from the 2026-07-07 design
session; P1/P2 phase text re-derived from the §8 decision log (originals not
retrieved verbatim). Everything else carries the session's substance.

## Current architecture and status

- **Deployment:** one self-hosted Go application with an embedded React UI,
  in-process zoekt serving, a supervised local SurrealDB child, and bounded
  index-builder/Buf children. Standalone is the supported development and
  small-deployment profile.
- **Source boundary:** repositories are read at immutable commits; mirrors and
  shards are derived and rebuildable, while database state and configuration
  are precious.
- **Shipped core:** repository sync, search and browsing, SCIP, Git history,
  authentication, permissions, audit, OpenAPI, MCP, and backup/restore.
- **Experimental annex:** Contract Atlas, Caller Map, Impact, Change Workbench,
  Thrift fields, and Kafka evidence are implemented but remain default-dark or
  fixture-bound. The retained external validation result is
  `NOT_ESTABLISHED`.
- **Microservice boundary:** the shipped service-scoped path admits at most one
  configured analysis unit per repository. It proves exact focused scope and a
  repository-wide caller overlay, but it is not yet a multi-service catalog or
  a whole-monorepo microservice product. The selected next product program makes
  many logical services first-class views over shared repository generations;
  it must not multiply a repository census, physical index, or source parse by
  the service count.
- **Current work:** Epic 30's single-node service-scope program is complete
  (2026-08-02). T30.1–T30.5 and the two post-T30.5 repairs shipped the strict
  analysis-unit contract, focused indexing, reusable candidate planning, and
  focused evidence. T30.6a–T30.6r shipped the independently bounded
  repository-overlay caller pipeline, authorized exact consumers, explicit
  retention decision, and the then-complete 52-component retention-status
  surface. T40.7 adds `evidence_chunk` as the 53rd component so its new durable
  accounting receipts remain operator-visible.
  T30.7 closes the epic with scope-aware Search, Contracts, Topics, Caller
  Map, Impact, and Workbench surfaces; coverage-certificate v3 and durable
  outcome receipts; exact caller record/progress summaries; HTTP/MCP scope
  parity; and a neutral ordinary-worker `make dev` cohort. T31.1 bounded
  pipeline diagnostics and T32.1's microservice-first program-contract closure
  completed on 2026-08-04. T32.2's authorized whole-monorepo baseline completed
  on 2026-08-04 with the source-free receipt at `spike/t322/results.json`; it
  establishes no SLO and selects no topology. T32.3's deterministic neutral
  authority/correctness corpus and 1,000/5,000-service load profiles completed
  on 2026-08-04. T32.4's source-free topology spike selected direct shared
  whole-repository shards for the initial v2 path on 2026-08-04. T32.5 closed
  the implementation gate with a conditional GO on 2026-08-04. T33.1's
  canonical service-catalog contract and T33.2 catalog ingestion/v1 migration
  completed on 2026-08-04. T33.3 independent service state, T33.4 authorized
  HTTP/MCP reads, and T33.5's accessible directory and neutral epic demo
  completed on 2026-08-05, closing Epic 33. T34.1's immutable repository
  source/search generation, T34.2's exact service-query compiler, T34.3's
  publication migration/recovery, and T34.4's shared All code/service product
  and neutral demo completed on 2026-08-05, closing Epic 34. T35.1's bounded
  generation-scoped chunk scheduler and T35.2's pin-aware lifecycle and
  retention decision, T35.3's bounded sweep/capacity control, and T35.4's
  lifecycle recovery demo completed on 2026-08-05, closing Epic 35. T36.1's
  bounded immutable Git reader and partition contract, T36.2's shared Go
  source-observation contract, T36.3's content-addressed observation
  publication, and T36.4's authorized progress/neutral multi-pack demo
  completed on 2026-08-05, closing Epic 36. T37.1's namespace-sharded
  declaration/resolver catalog, T37.2's RPC caller postings, T37.3's Kafka
  topic postings, T37.4's service projections and atomic relationship roots,
  and T37.5's exact readers, comparison, proof/Workbench integration, and
  neutral demo completed by 2026-08-06, closing Epic 37. T38.1's exact service
  overview, T38.2's cross-service explorer, T38.3's service-aware Impact and
  Workbench composition, T38.4's strict MCP parity, and T38.5's neutral
  product closure completed on 2026-08-06, closing Epic 38. T39.1's neutral
  correctness/admission/recovery gate completed on 2026-08-06. T39.2's
  separately authorized target operating-envelope run stopped on a terminal
  incremental pipeline failure on 2026-08-06. T39.3's independent
  security/lifecycle gate completed on 2026-08-06. T39.4's evidence-quality
  and workflow gate stopped before unsealing on 2026-08-06 because the public
  protocols remain unsealed design drafts. T39.5 recorded `DO_NOT_RELEASE`,
  kept all ten packs experimental-dark, and closed Epic 39 with continuation
  ineligible on 2026-08-06. T39.R1 subsequently closed the mirror-lock
  contention prerequisite without authorizing a target rerun. Epics 40–42 are
  scheduled as the next scale-convergence program: T40.1's closed refusal
  attribution/frozen neutral envelope and T40.2's independent derived-plan
  ownership completed on 2026-08-06, T40.3 is next, and Epic 41
  targets neutral authority for 10,000 accepted services with a floor of 8,000
  accepted services, and
  Epic 42 composes it with the very-large physical-repository envelope.
  Epics 25–28 remain unscheduled drafts.
  Production evidence registration remains gated and the distributed P6 fleet
  profile remains demand-driven.

Use [docs/ROADMAP.md](./docs/ROADMAP.md) for current sequencing,
[docs/BACKLOG.md](./docs/BACKLOG.md) for unscheduled draft acceptance criteria, and
[docs/BACKLOG_COMPLETED.md](./docs/BACKLOG_COMPLETED.md) for completed ticket
history. The decision table below is append-only. The later phase narrative
records the design's evolution and is not the active roadmap.

---

## 1. Decisions locked

| Area | Decision | Escape hatch |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-03 | Microservice-first product and system boundary | **Services, not repositories or files, are the primary product scope; repositories remain the physical source, authorization, and rebuild boundary.** phebs differentiates from general code search by producing versioned answers to service questions: what belongs to a service, which contracts it provides or uses, which other services may be affected, what evidence supports each relationship, and what remained unresolved. One repository may contain thousands of logical services. Stable logical service identity is separate from repository placement; the first program validates many services in one monorepo, while the catalog contract must not force service identity to equal repository or path identity. Catalog placements are commit-bound and carry explicit primary/supporting/shared/generated/typed/unowned path roles, many-to-many path membership, independent desired/current state, and separately visible ambiguity, conflict, failure, stale, and unavailable states. Service identity must come from a strict operator or committed authority and may be reconciled from build/catalog/deployment inputs; directory shape alone may produce only a proposal, never silent truth. One exact repository generation owns the streamed tree census and physical search corpus. All-code search reads that shared generation; service search applies the complete service predicate inside the zoekt query, never as a result-time filter. Source observations, declaration/resolver catalogs, and caller/topic postings are repository-shared and partitioned by bounded content/namespace keys; service projections join afterward. The intended steady-state cost is proportional to repository entries, distinct admitted source bytes, changed partitions, and emitted observations—not service count multiplied by repository bytes. One malformed or lagging service cannot relabel another service current, and an incomplete repository-wide relationship root cannot masquerade as complete. The first implementation remains single-node and preserves whole-repository behavior plus today's exact `analysis-unit-v1` identity through a side-by-side migration. | An index per service, a configuration-array loop over the current pipeline, result-time search filtering, guessed service identity, or a shared current bit for every service is rejected. Physical cohorting, incremental shard reuse, and the P6 fleet profile remain evidence-triggered topology choices. Cross-repository placement composition, additional languages/frameworks, and runtime overlays require their own versioned contracts and validation; the service identity chosen now must not preclude them. |
| 2026-08-03 | Multi-service validation and sequencing gate | **Measure before selecting the physical topology, but do not defer the multi-service product model.** T31.1 source-free diagnostics first observe one authorized target monorepo in whole-repository mode: index wall time/RSS/disk/shards, restart/no-op behavior, representative all-code queries, candidate planning, extraction, recovery, and retained-state growth are recorded against prospectively frozen host ceilings without retaining private paths, content, names, or infrastructure in this repository. A separate neutral correctness corpus freezes service membership, shared/generated/unowned paths, rename/delete/conflict cases, all-code and per-service query oracles, partial-currentness states, and cross-service relationship expectations; generated 1,000- and 5,000-service metadata/load profiles measure catalog, scheduler, reader, and retention cardinality without being mislabeled as a real-corpus SLO. Only then may a retained search-topology spike choose direct whole-repository shards, bounded cohorts, or a P6 escalation by proving result equality under broad queries, ranking ties, truncation, generation transitions, cold mmap/FD cost, and the target operating envelope. Before high-cardinality publications ship, terminal-job and artifact retention must have explicit bounded lifecycle/GC behavior that preserves proof/Investigation pins and active readers. Production evidence still requires pack-specific statistical validation and a separate pilot-continuation decision. The existing pilot remains one monorepo, one exact Go/gRPC migration, and one pinned baseline; it may supply private target measurements but is not broadened into a multi-service release claim. | A target index failure may accelerate cohort or fleet work but cannot weaken correctness, authorization, publication, or retention gates. A successful whole-repository index does not eliminate the catalog, independent per-service state, shared observation, relationship, or product-surface work. Numeric service/SLO limits become release claims only after their named retained measurements; synthetic results do not establish target-corpus scale. |
| 2026-08-03 | T31.1 bounded pipeline diagnostics | **Operational diagnosis is opt-in by component and remains outside evidence and publication authority.** `diagnostics.jobs` emits a source-free, 16-KiB-capped JSON receipt after each successfully persisted claim/start/terminal queue transition for every existing runner; it derives eligibility-relative queue wait from already loaded job timestamps and handler duration from one monotonic clock, adds no store call, and never includes arbitrary handler errors. `diagnostics.candidates` emits the indexed handoff and one 32-KiB-capped candidate-operation receipt distinguishing warm no-op, cold control reuse, marker recovery, repair, rebuild, and not-ready outcomes. Rebuild timing observes the existing tree walk, spool writes, caller sort/packing, file publication, control fingerprint, database transition, and marker cleanup; logical live/peak spool bytes are charged on existing writes/removals without a directory scan, stat, member reopen, hash pass, or new retained artifact. Plane counts and canonical/declared bytes come from the just-built or already strict-open manifest; pointer-only warm/cold reuse stays pointer-only and explicitly omits its manifest summary. `diagnostics.extraction` exposes already performed pointer/strict-open work, the ordered at-most-16-domain schedule, exact aggregate deferral trigger, durable outcome transitions, phase logs, and the existing 64-KiB operation receipt. `diagnostics.extractor_details` requires extraction diagnostics and adds at most 32 fixed, unique, lowercase scalar counters to the non-authoritative operation report only; gRPC, Thrift, and Kafka packs count their existing passes, recognized generated inputs/imports, inspected calls, resolved sites/topics, ambiguity/abstention, and skipped tests without collecting paths or samples. Durable outcome receipts, coverage manifests, generation identity, and proof bytes do not acquire these pack counters. Startup always emits the configured analysis-unit name/digest, path counts rather than paths, search/typed posture, enabled domains, and one bounded recommendation; an unconfigured installation emits the whole-repository posture. All diagnostics are restart-bound, default off except that startup posture is always on; sink failure/panic cannot affect queue state, retries, candidates, extraction, or publication. Steady state with switches off adds only boolean branches and bounded stack-scalar assignments, with no query, write, lock, corpus/shard read, Git/blob I/O, child, hash, filesystem scan, cache, or retained allocation. Enabled job diagnostics add bounded JSON formatting per persisted transition; enabled candidate diagnostics add monotonic observations and inline scalar accounting to work already performed; enabled extraction details add fixed scalar increments and at most 32 counters per domain. Concurrent workers independently multiply only that bounded formatting work. | Paths, source samples, raw errors, blob content, child output, dynamic counter names, more than 32 pack counters, persistent telemetry, new store reads for logging, or a diagnostic that participates in freshness/proof/publication requires a separate reviewed decision. `indexing.verbose` remains solely the zoekt-child output control. |
| 2026-08-04 | T32.2 authorized whole-monorepo baseline | **One completed source-free whole-repository baseline informs later topology work without becoming a scale claim.** The private authorized plan freezes ceilings, artifact digests, query battery, domain order, stop/teardown authority, and a commitment nonce; the checked-in receipt binds only an opaque run commitment plus closed public scalars for clone/fetch, index, restart, cold/warm search, candidate planning, ordered extraction outcomes, recovery, retention deltas, and teardown posture. Provisional packs stay off for indexing; candidate planning and extraction enable exactly one pack at a time; recovery interrupts a measured phase without weakening publication or authorization; teardown destroys derived installation data while recording whether the authorized source copy remains. Unknown fields, private identifiers, paths, queries, host/tool identity, raw errors, and credentials are rejected. Claims explicitly deny SLO, accuracy, topology selection, and multi-service release authority. No production runtime behavior changes. | A second target, different host class, or production code change requires a new nonce, authorization review, and freeze. Numeric release limits remain unselected until later Epic 32 tickets. |
| 2026-08-04 | T32.3 independent neutral service oracle | **Topology work receives one deterministic public correctness oracle and two explicit synthetic load inputs without preselecting the production catalog schema.** A five-commit neutral Git history freezes distinct and restricted services; primary, supporting, shared, generated, typed, and unowned roles; many-to-many membership; accepted, proposed, conflicting, malformed, and tombstoned authority; stable-identity placement rename; split, merge, removal, stale, partial, unavailable, and conflict states; exact literal All code/service query results; and resolved/unresolved RPC and Kafka relationships. The independently authored oracle consumes no phebs result. Complete 1,000- and 5,000-service inputs freeze respectively 3,151/15,751 file records, 5,000/25,000 memberships, five equal role populations, shared fan-out 10, contract fan-out 25, and exact generated content cardinality/bytes. Two-run tests require byte-identical catalogs, oracles, profiles, Git commits, trees, bundle, receipt, and artifact digests. `t323-neutral-authority-input-v1` is fixture vocabulary only; production packages never import the generator. | T32.4 may consume these retained oracles and profiles for equality and cost measurements but cannot reinterpret them as target scale, extraction accuracy, a topology choice, or T33 wire authority. Any fixture semantic change advances its schema/receipt and pinned expectations. |
| 2026-08-04 | T32.4 initial direct shared-search topology | **GO with one direct whole-repository zoekt generation per exact repository revision set for the initial multi-service v2 path.** T32.2 completed its prospectively frozen direct envelope, so the preregistered cohort trigger did not fire. T32.3's five-revision oracle passed all 40 authority/query cases: 33 exact or stale searches executed, seven restricted/removed/unavailable cases were checked against catalog visibility or unavailable service state before search, All code and catalog-derived service results were exact, and every service filename predicate was inside the branch-bound zoekt query before ranking and top-K. Broad raw-byte oracles, adversarial all-document ranking, top-K truncation, revision binding, per-revision catalog/oracle equality, repeatable prior-revision selection, and static `A → B → A` branch selection within one immutable shard set passed. The spike did not interrupt publication or transition physical generations. The complete 1,000/5,000-service synthetic profiles built as one shard each; one service predicate remained 369 canonical query-tree bytes, while compiling every service measured aggregate metadata cost rather than a request path. Direct shards are GO to T32.5; bounded prefix-aware/hybrid cohorts and P6 are NO-GO because their named trigger was not met, not because they are impossible. | Reopen cohorts or P6 only after a named direct-topology correctness or capacity limit fails under a prospectively frozen experiment. T32.4's synthetic ctags-disabled timings and service counts establish no target SLO, accuracy, release limit, or multi-service release authority. T32.5 still freezes identities, migration, initial limits with margins, and explicit deferrals before implementation. |
| 2026-08-04 | T32.5 multi-service v2 implementation gate | **CONDITIONAL GO to dependency-ordered Epics 33–39; no runtime registration or release is authorized.** A service is the repository-scoped pair of one stable 128-byte-or-shorter logical key using the existing `analysis-unit-v1` token syntax and a nonrepeating incarnation; display name, repository path, authority record, and placement may change without silently changing that identity, while key replacement is an explicit tombstone/successor transition. The repository identity retains its existing incarnation. A source generation binds that repository identity, exact revision set, and one streamed census; a search generation binds the source generation, selected direct topology policy, and complete physical root. A catalog generation binds exactly one accepted base authority, any explicit operator override, the source generation, and the canonical complete accepted/proposal/conflict/rejected/unowned projection. A service desired generation binds its incarnation plus exact catalog record; capability-specific active generations bind the exact source, catalog, policy, and complete publication roots that produced them. Semantic generation IDs are domain-separated SHA-256 digests over closed canonical inputs; store-minted incarnations and monotonic publication revisions separately fence `A → B → A`, so equal endpoint values never erase an intervening transition. **Authority is explicit and non-guessing:** one repository selects either an exact-commit committed catalog or an operator-owned versioned catalog; multiple selected bases are conflict, not precedence. A versioned operator override may explicitly add, exclude, merge, split, rename, or correct roles over that one base. Detector/build/deployment/directory outputs remain provenance-bearing proposals until explicitly accepted. Closed-wire, unsafe-path, successor-cycle, duplicate, and limit violations reject the whole input before a replacement exists; well-formed but unaccepted semantic claims become explicit proposal, conflict, or rejected records, so unrelated accepted services and the shared source/search corpus remain usable. The deterministic legacy path is used only when no v2 authority is selected: the configured `analysis-unit-v1` name becomes the repository-scoped service key, its exact digest is retained, its primary/supporting membership and typed designation are preserved, and v1/v2 namespaces remain side by side until exact v2 replacements exist. No partial or failed replacement relabels v1 authority, and no v1/proof-pinned bytes are reclaimed by this gate. Membership authority is an ordered set of `(service key, canonical path, role)` records: `primary`, `supporting`, `shared`, `generated`, and `typed` are explicit nonexclusive roles, so one path may carry multiple roles and belong to many services; `unowned` is the explicit repository complement and may not also have accepted membership. Search collapses roles to the exact authorized path set and places it inside the exact branch-bound zoekt query; the physical topology remains one direct whole-repository shard generation, with cohorts and P6 reopening only after a named retained failure. Each capability reports `current`, `stale`, `unavailable`, `conflict`, or `removed` independently. An authorized stale service may serve only its last complete active generation with both desired and active identities; it never becomes current by age or sibling success. A named all-services-at-one-source result is unavailable until every included service is exact against the same source/catalog roots. **Initial v2 admission is deliberately below the retained 5,000-service Darwin arm64/Go 1.26.5 synthetic envelope:** at most 4,000 service records, 20,000 membership triples, 12,000 distinct membership-or-unowned paths, accepted-service path fan-out 20, and 5 MiB of encoded and canonical catalog bytes; these reserve respectively 20%, 20%, 23.8%, 20%, and 5.6% beneath the measured 5,000/25,000/15,750/25/5,551,129 maxima. The profile contains 15,751 file records because `go.mod` has neither membership nor unowned classification. Every dimension binds independently and establishes only a refusal boundary, not supported target scale. Each service also inherits `analysis-unit-v1`'s at-most-128 selected paths, 64-KiB aggregate selected-path bytes, 4,096-byte canonical path limit, and 128-byte key/name limit; those are integrity bounds, not newly measured multi-service capacity. T33.1 must prove maximum-shape decode/canonicalization allocation and may reduce, but not increase, these caps without a new retained decision. T33.2 owns side-by-side ingestion/migration; T33.3 owns independent state/incarnation/tombstones; T34.1–T34.3 own direct physical publication, exact query predicates, and real generation/recovery transitions; T35 owns bounded scheduling, terminal-job/artifact retention, pins, GC, and disk pressure before Epics 36–37 multiply artifacts; T36–T37 own parse-once observations and relationship indexes; T38 owns product surfaces; T39 owns target-specific authority confirmation, commit-frequency/catch-up/query operating gates, pack accuracy, workflow value, and any shadow/advisory release decision. The first milestone is single-node. Cross-repository service composition, automatic authority adapters, additional language/framework/client families, runtime overlays, incremental zoekt metadata rebinding, cohorts, and P6 are explicit future gates.** T32.2 remains one environment-bound source-free target observation; T32.3/T32.4 remain synthetic mechanics evidence. None establishes an SLO, accuracy, completeness, migration completion, decommission safety, or multi-service release, and `GATE2-V2` remains `NOT_ESTABLISHED`. | Stop the dependent ticket if its maximum admitted shape cannot remain bounded or preserve the exact prior authority. Exceeding an admission cap requires a prospectively frozen larger neutral profile and a new dated limit decision; a target or availability requirement that breaks direct single-node behavior reopens cohorts/P6 without weakening authority, equality, recovery, or retention gates. |
| 2026-08-04 | T33.1 canonical service-catalog contract | **`phebs-service-catalog-v2` is the closed repository-local authority projection; it is a pure contract and creates no selected production authority.** One catalog names exactly one base authority: `committed` uses a full lowercase 40- or 64-digit Git object ID and `operator` uses a bounded explicit version token. One optional versioned operator override may be selected; every service, membership, and unowned record names `base` or `override`, and override provenance is invalid without that selected override. Stable service keys use the existing 128-byte `analysis-unit-v1` token syntax and remain distinct from display names and placement paths. Services are explicit `accepted`, `proposal`, `conflict`, or `rejected` records; nonaccepted states carry a bounded reason, accepted records require at least one primary membership, and rejected records alone may name an acyclic, bounded successor graph. Membership is the duplicate-free ordered set of `(service_key,path,role)` records with nonexclusive `primary|supporting|shared|generated|typed` roles. Exact path and role reuse across services is legal; typed requires an exact supporting record, same-service distinct prefix overlap is invalid, and unowned paths may not overlap an accepted placement. T33.2 binds this complete projection to an exact source census and proves that explicit unowned entries are its complement; T33.1 neither reads Git nor guesses omitted paths. The strict token decoder rejects unknown, duplicate, missing, null, wrongly typed, or trailing values and checks collection dimensions before append. It retains T32.5's 4,000 services, 20,000 memberships, 12,000 distinct membership-or-unowned paths, exact accepted-path fan-out 20, 5-MiB encoded/canonical bytes, and inherited per-service 128-path/64-KiB path-byte/4,096-byte path/128-byte key-and-name bounds. It additionally bounds authority IDs and versions at 128 bytes, reasons at 512 bytes, and aggregate successor edges at 4,000. Canonical identity sorts services, successors, memberships, and unowned paths, emits bounded closed JSON, and hashes `"phebs-service-catalog-v2\x00" || canonical_bytes` with SHA-256. Maximum-shape tests exercise exactly 4,000 accepted services, 20,000 role records, 12,000 distinct paths, fan-out 20, strict decode, canonicalization, and digest. The package is not imported by a runtime owner, so requests, queries, startup/restart, sync ticks, retries/no-ops, and publication transitions add no work.** | T33.2 may ingest only a fully validated catalog and owns repository/source-generation binding, census-complement proof, immutable publication, and exact `analysis-unit-v1` migration. T33.3 separately adds incarnation and lifecycle state. A new authority kind, role, automatic adapter, cross-repository placement, runtime registration, or higher cap requires its own reviewed decision. |
| 2026-08-04 | T33.2 catalog ingestion and v1 migration | **One explicit `service_catalogs[repository]` selection binds a normalized local JSON file as either exact-HEAD `committed` authority or opaque-versioned `operator` authority; there is no build, deployment, or directory adapter.** Configuration repeats the selected authority ID and, for operator input, version; committed input declares the repository's exact indexed HEAD as its catalog version. `Committed` is asserted provenance in T33.2: its operator-supplied bytes are not read from or compared with a repository blob, and an in-repository reader remains a separate automatic-adapter decision. Both kinds use an absolute clean non-symlink regular file, and decoded authority must equal the selection. When no v2 selection exists, an already committed `analysis-unit-v1` index state deterministically imports as one accepted service: its name is the key/display name, its exact digest is retained as both legacy namespace and authority version, primary/supporting memberships and exact typed designation are preserved, and every other regular source file becomes an explicit exact unowned entry within the unchanged 12,000-path cap. Removing a v2 selection when that v1 state exists publishes the deterministic v1 import as a real current-pointer transition rather than retaining v2 precedence. The v1 repository/index state remains readable and no old generation is reclaimed. Each new catalog generation streams one exact indexed-commit `git ls-tree -rz` census, admits only canonical regular files, binds mode/blob OID/path into a domain-separated census digest, requires every catalog membership/unowned placement to resolve, and proves every regular file is accepted or unowned but never both. Proposal/conflict/rejected memberships must resolve but do not become accepted coverage; proposal-only files therefore still require unowned placement. The immutable generation identity binds repository, source kind/path/commit, census digest/counts, authority/override, catalog digest, and optional v1 digest; a separate store-minted current revision/time fences `A → B → A`. One transaction fences the repository's indexed HEAD and v1 digest, rejects same authority/override version with different catalog bytes, creates or exact-reuses the immutable generation, and only then advances the current pointer. Invalid input, census gaps, stale HEAD, version reuse, and persistence failure leave the prior pointer unchanged. Startup reconciles repositories independently and continues past per-repository refusal; an index completion retries that repository after the existing candidate handoff. Exact source/catalog retries perform only strict point reads plus the selected catalog-file read for v2 (metadata-only for v1) and do not rescan Git. Canonical catalog bytes and current authority are precious SurrealDB state, survive export/import byte-for-byte, and are not cleared with derived candidate/resolver/caller pointers.** | T33.3 owns service incarnation, desired/active state, explicit removal/tombstones, and bounded history summaries. T33.4 owns authorized reads. Automatic authority adapters, a third authority kind, nonregular source membership, cross-repository placement, retained-generation GC, runtime registration, and higher caps require separate reviewed decisions. |
| 2026-08-05 | T33.3 independent service desired/active state | **One `phebs-service-state-v1` point row per repository-local service key makes lifecycle independent without turning the whole catalog digest into every service's identity.** The desired generation is a domain-separated SHA-256 binding of repository, exact source generation, and only the normalized service record plus that service's memberships; the source generation separately binds repository, indexed commit, census digest, and file count. Authority-version, unowned, and sibling-only changes therefore advance the repository catalog/summary fence without relabeling an unchanged service, while a source or exact local-record change advances that service's desired generation. A store-minted row control revision fences `A → B → A`; incarnation begins at one, survives ordinary edits and removal, and increments only when a tombstoned key is re-added. Rejected records become explicit tombstones with their successor edges; omission tombstones a previously live key with an explicit removal reason; re-add clears the prior incarnation's active identity. Accepted rows are `unavailable` without an active generation, `stale` when active differs from desired, and `current` only when active desired/source identities are exact; proposal is unavailable, conflict is explicit, and removed is never served. An active catalog generation may remain an older catalog generation only when the service-local desired and exact source generation are unchanged, which is the sibling-isolation rule rather than inferred freshness. One compare-and-swap activation changes only that row and summary counts. A `phebs-service-state-repository-v1` point summary binds the exact catalog generation and catalog control revision, carries independently digested bounded counts, and serializes transitions. Catalog publication remains the T33.2 transaction; the following state transaction updates at most 4,000 desired rows plus at most 4,000 live prior rows atomically. The narrow crash/failure gap is fail closed because summary/detail reads reject a catalog/summary mismatch, and the exact startup/index retry repairs it. Exact no-op reconciliation revalidates at most the admitted 5-MiB catalog and reads only catalog/summary points; a strict service detail read still validates the admitted catalog but hashes and allocates only the requested service projection, and activation reuses one catalog/summary snapshot rather than nesting detail, summary, and catalog opens. A real transition performs no Git/blob/shard read or child, retains maps bounded by the admitted current catalog/live rows, point-reads only current desired tombstones, and never scans immutable catalog generations or historical tombstones. Rows, summary digests, revisions, timestamps, active identities, incarnations, and tombstones are precious SurrealDB state and survive backup/restore exactly. No HTTP, MCP, search, relationship, or UI surface is registered.** | T33.4 owns authorization-first paged/detail reads over these rows. T34.3 owns validation of real physical active-generation transitions; T35 owns retained catalog-generation GC without deleting current service tombstones. A larger service cap, automatic incarnation inference across keys, tombstone reclamation, or weaker mismatch behavior requires a new reviewed decision. |

| 2026-07-28 | T30.4 streamed candidate planning and extraction admission | **Every enabled extraction is now admitted by one strictly validated, commit-bound candidate publication before an extraction run can begin.** A durable `candidate_manifest_job` stage follows indexing and streams the authoritative HEAD tree once under the repository lock. Before the census it rejects external Git object alternates and non-commit inputs; every leaf must have an explicitly supported mode, while exact gitlinks remain census-only repository boundaries even when a policy-shaped path matches an extractor suffix. The census binds the exact repository, indexed commit, committed analysis-unit digest when present, complete regular-file/gitlink/symlink counts and digests, versioned domain-enumeration and symlink policies, and per-domain repository/unit counts, bytes, and digests while retaining rows only for selected candidates. Repository/local candidates are written to bounded canonical NDJSON members; repository-overlay caller candidates use `SHA-256("phebs-caller-path-v1\0" || UTF8(path))`, begin at two prefix bits, and recursively split by the next bit until every nonempty leaf contains at most 4,096 records and 64 MiB of declared blob bytes. Blob OID and size change manifest identity but not path assignment; an oversized singleton or an unsplittable 256-bit bucket refuses. Unit membership is precomputed on each applicable record, but T30.4 deliberately feeds the existing repository-wide extractor view: T30.5 alone may narrow local evidence and key it by unit. Broad enumeration-only symlinks are skipped; a required alias must resolve through at most 16 immutable Git-object links to a regular path selected and required by the same domain, and fixed `index.scip`/attribution roots are unconditionally regular. An invalid required or fixed alias is deliberately a shared-planner integrity refusal that blocks the one publication and therefore all configured domains before extraction; after admission, ordinary parser and domain-publication failures retain T19.8's per-domain isolation. Publication stages and syncs generation-digest-prefixed members with exact content digests, creates the stable repository `.publishing` marker, replaces members and renames `phebs-candidate-<sha256(repository)>.manifest.json` last, atomically advances the database pointer while ensuring one pending extraction successor, then removes the marker. Retry reuses the identical publication; a same-HEAD/unit/policy conflict is planner nondeterminism. Open refuses a marker, absent/partial/extra/duplicate/malformed member, digest or policy disagreement, stale HEAD/unit, database/filesystem-pointer mismatch, or special file before `BeginExtractionRun`; cross-plane identity validation uses a bounded, cancellable external merge whose package-owned scratch is removed before exact membership validation and recovered after crashes. Startup and repository cleanup remove orphan stages/publications and requeue current work; a malformed derived database pointer is cleared under the repository lock and rebuilt, while store infrastructure failures remain fail-closed. `$DATA/candidates` is derived, excluded from backup, and rebuilt after restore. The retained [prospective planner measurement](./spike/t304/README.md) streamed 200,008 regular files into five repository rows and six caller rows across three two-bit leaves (`00:1`, `10:3`, `11:2`), staged five files totaling 13,049 bytes, bounded planner spool/split scratch at 4,134 bytes and validation scratch at 3,514 bytes for 17,183 bytes conservative peak candidate disk, and produced byte-identical output in 3.55 s/3.43 s with peak RSS of 61,145,088/60,801,024 bytes. It freezes local gates at 10 s wall time, 256 MiB peak RSS, and 16 MiB peak candidate disk including publication plus the higher planner or validation scratch phase. This changes planning and admission, not evidence identity, typed-index posture, production registration, runtime-use, completeness, extraction-accuracy, migration-completion, or decommission-safety claims. |
| 2026-07-28 | T30.3 focused zoekt publication and recovery boundary | **A configured analysis unit is now the physical zoekt input, and one exact manifest-bound shard generation is the only searchable publication.** The same-source-line `phebs-focused-index` child resolves every selected file/root independently at HEAD and each allowlisted branch/tag commit, rejects a missing selection or symlink/gitlink/special entry in any lane, groups identical immutable path/blob pairs across revisions, and feeds only those bytes to the pinned upstream `index.Builder`; repositories without `analysis_units` retain `zoekt-git-index`. A trusted counting reader checks each path/blob pair against the selected tree census before `git cat-file`, then reports successful opened-blob count/bytes and refused out-of-unit attempts; a nonzero refusal makes the child fail. Search checks the marker and revalidates committed state, exact revision metadata, unit/generation identities, member/sidecar bytes, and the absence of extra repository shards on every admission; partial, stale, mixed, missing, extra, or state-uncommitted generations therefore receive an empty RepoSet even though zoekt continues watching the shared directory. A fresh equivalent build is compared by unit/generation/search semantics, never by publication digest, because upstream builder time/identity is content. Non-empty leaf prefixes are prefix-free, disjoint, and ordered by numeric hash range; records inside a leaf are ordered by hash, path bytes, then blob OID. No leaf is visible independently: publication requires every declared leaf at the identical repository commit, unit digest, declaration-set digest, candidate-manifest digest, resolver-catalog digest, and extractor generation. | 2026-07-27 | Epic 27 document schema-manifest evidence specification | **A private document-store dialect does not enter phebs; a committed employer-neutral structural manifest may.** The unscheduled round-one proposal defines strict JSON `phebs-document-schema-v1` as the only declaration boundary. Associations are not foreign keys; views do not prove deployment or freshness; either source class proves only the committed bytes. Missing bytes yield a request-schema-export workflow state. Round one is a declaration-only schema set → table → nested-field catalog and explicit two-snapshot structural comparison; it does not populate the relationship-only Workbench resource plane, bind usage, compare with a live store, or fold multiple snapshots into a claimed current shape. | 2026-07-25 | Epic 21 Change Workbench boundary | **Why → What → Where → How is one Investigation-native workflow, not a new evidence or authorization system.** Why is human authority: problem, desired outcome, success criteria, non-goals, assumptions, and acceptance are immutable revisioned inputs that phebs cannot infer from code. What selects exact contract identities or commits to bounded proposed IDL sources through the existing pure-reader/sandbox boundaries. Where composes Atlas, compatibility, Epic 20 Caller Map/comparison, field/implementation evidence, unit attribution, and an adjacent Analysis scope & gaps receipt; source, contract, service, owner, message/topic, datastore resource, and runtime observation remain typed entities, never aliases. Kafka, Redis, document-store, SQL, and runtime planes render unsupported until separately reviewed evidence packs exist. How presents cited source/history and unaccepted checklist suggestions; only audited immutable human Dispositions in the fixed accepted/rejected/completed/reopened/waived vocabulary affect the checklist projection, corrections supersede rather than edit, and checklist completion never establishes migration completion or retirement safety. The user vocabulary distinguishes matching static evidence, declaration-proven resolved callers, name matches needing review, extractor abstentions, and the canonical Coverage certificate behind the human-facing Analysis scope & gaps summary. One versioned glossary drives or guards UI help, API vocabulary, MCP descriptions, and MANUAL prose; every qualified section has a hover/focus/tap accessible help popover. | 2026-07-22 | T10.4 bounded multi-revision indexing | **HEAD stays implicit and authoritative; extra revisions are explicit search-only scopes.** `revisions` maps a canonical repository name to at most seven case-sensitive query selectors and full `refs/heads/*` / `refs/tags/*` refs, keeping the PLAN ceiling at eight revisions including HEAD; wildcard, short/ambiguous, commit-id, duplicate, and unsafe refs fail config admission. Each index job peels the complete admitted set before invoking the same-SHA `zoekt-git-index` child once, publishes deterministic `(selector, shard branch, commit)` state atomically with the legacy HEAD commit, and short-circuits only when the whole set matches. Startup reconciliation compares exact shard branch/version metadata to that state. Search strips exactly one bare `rev:` scope before native query parsing, applies principal visibility first, then compiles visible repositories into exact per-branch scopes; result commits are checked against the selected persisted revision before serialization. Unqualified search always adds exact `HEAD`, so enabling revisions cannot leak feature-branch content into existing queries. Local watch fingerprints HEAD plus only allowlisted refs; remote sync/webhook jobs already rebuild on fetched ref movement. File/source links remain immutable commits. Extraction, SCIP defaults, coverage, and proof bundles deliberately remain HEAD-bound rather than silently multiplying evidence semantics. |

Failed imports deliberately leave a partial target that later restores refuse. Recovery config parsing validates structure while preserving unexpanded secret references. Mirrors/shards remain excluded: ordinary startup reconcile → sync → index is the sole rebuild path. The prior cold-copy path remains supported. |

| 2026-07-22 | T14.4 proof-bundle retention | **Opt-in age bound outside content identity; bundle deletion only releases ownership.** `proof_bundles.retention` is disabled by default (`"0"` or omission) and, when set to a positive Go duration, bounds a bundle from its most recent successful materialization. That lifecycle timestamp is store metadata outside canonical `proof-bundle-v1` JSON, so repeated answers remain byte-identical and keep the same `pb_<sha256>` ID even as an active bundle refreshes its lifetime; pre-T14.4 rows fall back to their existing creation timestamp and are therefore not immortal after opt-in. Reads apply the same cutoff before authorization and return the indistinguishable not-found response for expired, missing, and unauthorized IDs. A bounded boot/hourly sweeper transaction rechecks the cutoff, deletes one bundle, and deletes only evidence pins whose kind is exactly `proof-bundle:<id>`; other bundle and checkpoint pins cannot match. It never deletes extraction evidence. The existing proof-aware evidence sweeper remains the sole reclaimer and can collect a superseded run only after its final pin is gone. This supersedes T14.1's interim indefinite-lifetime clause and closes the before-pilot gate. |
| 2026-07-22 | T14.3 pinned Buf compatibility child | **Commodity spec verdict, phebs-owned evidence join, no corpus execution.** `check_contract_compatibility` accepts a canonical contract lineage plus bounded before/after `.proto` source sets, commits to sorted path/content digests, and runs only Buf v1.72.0's `breaking` command under the fixed `WIRE` policy with structured output and symlink traversal disabled. Buf is a go.mod tool and sibling binary like `zoekt-git-index`; phebs rejects a mismatched version before registering either transport. Inputs are validated (canonical relative paths, UTF-8, 256 files/side, 4 MiB/file, 32 MiB aggregate) and copied into a fresh 0700 tree; no repository binary, script, plugin, generator, or config is invoked. The host boundary denies network and writes outside that tree and enforces wall/CPU/output limits plus a 512 MiB memory ceiling (Linux namespace + virtual-memory rlimit; macOS Seatbelt + process-group RSS watchdog); an unsupported sandbox fails closed and leaves the Huma operation/MCP tool undiscoverable. Canonical `proof-bundle-v1` adds an optional compatibility result: input commitments, exact Buf rule/message/span, affected stable field identities, and a deterministic bundle-local `extraction_run` record containing engine, pinned version, exact relative arguments, exit, and result. This is deliberately not a row in the repository extraction publication table: caller-supplied input sets have no authorized indexed repository revision, and publishing them would corrupt latest-run coverage semantics. Each affected `(lineage,message,field_number)` becomes an explicit `scip-proto-field` filter in the existing shared proof builder; permission filtering, coverage confirmation, atom resolution, persistence, reauthorization, and MCP projection remain one engine. A WIRE verdict applies only to the committed inputs; evidence-derived consumer inventory remains provisional and cannot establish absence, migration completeness, or decommissioning safety. |
| 2026-07-22 | T14.2 proof-backed MCP projection | **One proof engine, two transports; dark means undiscoverable.** T14.1's query entry points are a shared in-process `api.ProofService`; Huma and MCP call that same builder with the request context, so the agent surface cannot reproduce, weaken, or drift from permission filtering, canonical identities, evidence resolution, coverage confirmation, bundle persistence, or visibility-context construction. When `experimental.provisional_proto_extraction` is enabled, the stateless `/api/mcp` server adds `find_operation_consumers`, `find_proto_field_references`, and `get_extraction_coverage`, each returning the complete `proof-bundle-v1` envelope as structured content with source occurrences as citations and the provisional no-conclusions caveat. When disabled, the proof service is nil and those tools are not registered or discoverable. `check_contract_compatibility` deliberately remains absent until T14.3 supplies the pinned Buf engine and a real input/output contract; a permanently failing placeholder would falsely advertise a capability and freeze a speculative schema. Acceptance runs one official-SDK stateless Streamable HTTP session through both consumer tools plus coverage, proves exact citations/coverage/principal, and adversarially verifies no hidden-repository evidence call or serialized name. |
| 2026-07-22 | T14.1 permission-safe query proof bundles | **Content-addressed answers over the caller's authorized universe; possession is never authorization.** The experimental evidence surface now exposes `find_operation_consumers`, `find_proto_field_references`, and `get_extraction_coverage`. Each successful query constructs canonical `proof-bundle-v1` JSON containing the canonical question, complete bounded matching assertions and resolved evidence atoms/occurrences, the T13.3 coverage certificate, explicit extractor/run bindings, the provisional-evidence caveat, and a `visibility_context` whose permission snapshot commits to the stable principal, authorization-provider generation, and visible-repository-set digest. Permission filtering precedes every evidence read; therefore an invisible repository cannot affect assertion selection, counts, coverage, or content identity, and different principals/visible universes produce different sha256-derived bundle IDs. Construction reads the certificate before and after evidence resolution and retries on a changed digest, then atomically persists immutable content and pins every published run it names. A bundle read takes one current authorized-repository snapshot and requires every repository in its full visible-universe scope to remain present and authorized before decoding or returning content; missing, deleting, renamed, or newly invisible repositories produce the same not-found response, including for the creator, so IDs are not bearer credentials and revocation applies retroactively. Bounded query refusals are `422`, never partial answers. The store deliberately treats bundle JSON as opaque and exposes no unauthorised content read surface. Its interim bundle/pin lifetime was indefinite; the T14.4 ADR above supersedes that lifecycle clause. The routes remain disabled with `experimental.provisional_proto_extraction`; MCP projection and compatibility are governed by the later T14.2/T14.3 ADRs above. |
| 2026-07-22 | T13.3 per-answer coverage certificate | **Deterministic visible-universe honesty record; latest attempts are distinct from published evidence.** `extract.BuildCoverageCertificate` compiles an already-authorized visible repository slice × requested domains into `coverage-certificate-v1`: per repository the indexed revision and revision-correct SCIP availability (published `scip`/`scip-index-absent` protocols count only when the run commit equals the indexed commit); per domain the exact published run id, extractor, commit, freshness, sorted protocols, complete bounded source scope (corpus/candidate/read files, bytes, digest), unresolved/assertion/atom counts, and the latest attempt's id/input/extractor/status/failure. The published coverage-failure array remains represented for exactness but is empty under the current atomic publisher, which rejects partial failures. `extraction_attempt` is one durable non-evidence marker per repository/domain: begin replaces it, publish advances it, abort marks it, and sweeping a killed staged run marks it aborted before deleting run evidence. Therefore a same-commit forced replacement or extractor upgrade that fails changes the certificate while the prior fresh publication correctly remains query-visible. The sha256 digest covers canonical time-free JSON with the digest field empty; publication/attempt timestamps are excluded, so equal logical state yields byte-equal certificates. The builder consumes only the narrow published-run + latest-attempt `RunSource` read surface and the visible slice: invisible repositories are never queried, named, or counted, pinned by an adversarial non-interference test that mutates hidden state and compares certificate bytes. Visibility filtering remains the caller's authorization boundary (T10.3 pattern); API/proof-bundle exposure is T14.1. |
| 2026-07-22 | T13.2 SCIP proto-field reference identity and reader boundary | **Committed SCIP evidence, exact source spans, version-independent field identity.** The pure-reader SDK gains only an optional `ReadSCIPIndex` capability fixed to the repository-root `index.scip` and its existing 64 MiB ceiling; arbitrary source reads and fact spans remain capped at 10 MiB, and a fact still cites the immediately preceding trusted source read. The extractor streams the immutable index, binds global SCIP symbols to generated protobuf field/getter definitions using exact ranges plus generated struct tags and the generator's source-proto declaration, and emits `REFERENCES_PROTO_FIELD` only for non-definition occurrences with valid source ranges. Canonical identity is `(contract_lineage_id, message_full_name, field_number)`: lineage hashes SCIP scheme + package manager + package name while deliberately excluding dependency version and accessor/field name, so a same-number rename across consumer dependency versions remains one identity; missing package identity, ambiguous declaration/binding, local symbols, malformed ranges, or unavailable source declarations abstain instead of guessing. Each assertion subject is occurrence-scoped (`path:start-end`) so distinct read/write uses in one file cannot collapse into a conflicting semantic assertion. Classification records SCIP's bitset with deterministic precedence `write > read > test > generated > unknown`, while `code_role` independently preserves test/generated/source placement for query filtering. The extractor remains behind `experimental.provisional_proto_extraction`; a committed index is product input, never generated or downloaded by phebs. Post-review remediation (same PR): struct tags parse via stdlib `reflect.StructTag` (pure-reader allowlist gains `reflect`, tag-string parsing only), and UTF-8 positions reject mid-rune byte offsets for parity with the UTF-16 surrogate rule. |
| 2026-07-22 | T13.1 grpcgo review remediation and completion | **FIXED / COMPLETE (dark scope).** Extractor `1.1.0` turns every parse, size, call-name, and registration-name abstention into an exact-span `tier=unresolved` assertion so the trusted T12.2 worker can reconcile and publish `UnresolvedCount`; coverage counts distinct semantic gaps while repeated source occurrences remain separate supporting atoms. The repo-wide index retains `(service FQN, stub path, provisional lineage)` candidates and resolves only singleton sets: generated helper-name collisions and duplicate FQNs across independent paths abstain rather than overwrite or merge. Protobuf full names now accept the valid one-segment, package-less form. A real-worker regression proves two ambiguous occurrences publish as one unresolved assertion with two atoms; focused tests pin helper collisions, path-lineage separation, package-less services, exact spans, and deterministic output. Typed receiver resolution and canonical cross-repository lineage remain pilot/T13.2 promotion work; the default-disabled posture and no-conclusions gate are unchanged. |
| 2026-07-22 | T13.1 grpcgo consumer extractor (dark scope) | **Syntactic port of the spike's validated rules into the pure-reader SDK; typed fidelity deferred to the pilot gate.** New `internal/extract/extractors/grpcgo`: pass 1 indexes the repo's generated `*_grpc.pb.go` stubs (ServiceDesc `ServiceName` literals, anchored full-method constants, `Register<X>Server` declarations — never blob retention, only derived names, preserving the SDK's one-blob streaming contract); pass 2 emits `REGISTERS_GRPC_SERVICE` (tier `derived`) and `CALLS_OPERATION` (tier `heuristic`, method name unique across the stub index; ambiguity abstains into `UnresolvedCount` rather than guessing). `code_role` precedence ported verbatim from the spike's validated classifier; lineage reuses the T12.3 provisional recipe pending T13.2 promotion. The spike's go/types resolution requires exec + hydrated module caches, which the pure-reader invariant forbids — the honest lower tiers are recorded on every assertion, and the re-scoped dark AC (BACKLOG f5d3806) defers the blind-evaluation thresholds to the pilot's internal gate. Registry pin test updated: opt-in flag now enables protodecl + grpcgo, default remains disabled. Tests: fixture corpus, exact-span citation, ambiguity abstention, role precedence rows, unparseable/oversized abstention, two-run byte-identical determinism (the campaign's standard as a unit test), and a no-accuracy-language guard. |

| Branch scope | **HEAD-only by default** + explicit per-repo allowlist capped ≈8; feature branches never indexed; monorepo freshness via debounced cadence, zoekt delta builds as the lever | — |

### 1.1 Why upstream-only dissolves shard compatibility

The port owns both sides of the shard boundary: our binary reads shards, and our
container builds the writer from the *same module version*
(`go build github.com/sourcegraph/zoekt/cmd/zoekt-git-index`). Compatibility with
fork-written shards stops mattering — reindex at cutover.

### 1.2 Fleet profile (P6)

N homogeneous replicas of the same binary. Each owns a rendezvous-hashed repo
subset: indexes it, serves it from local NVMe shards. gRPC scatter-gather across
replicas for queries; SurrealDB cut over from the supervised local child to an
external server deployment (export/import);
owner-scoped job claims; admission control with **separate interactive and agent
traffic budgets**. Standalone remains the dev/small default; fleet lands last so
single-node correctness is proved first.

| 2026-08-05 | T34.3 search publication migration and recovery | **Whole-repository indexing is now single-write v2 while reads remain explicitly dual-mode: All code may open a legacy v1 whole publication only when no v2 search root exists, but a service query can open only a complete exact v2 source/search/whole generation.** The presence of a malformed, interrupted, stale, or mismatched v2 root is a refusal and never permission to fall back or relabel legacy/focused results. Runtime opening authorization-checks first, strict-opens the current state summary and service row, loads the exact historical active catalog for a stale service, binds T34.2 to the current v2 controls and an exact whole-cache lease, and repeats store, repository, revision, control-root, and lease fences after zoekt returns. Any concurrent catalog/state/index publication, restore, rollback, deletion, or replacement discards the response. One bounded generation reconciler verifies current catalog HEAD/file-count equality, performs control-only no-op checks, fully validates every source member and shard before a real transition, then atomically activates all accepted noncurrent services against one catalog/source/search snapshot; proposal/conflict/removed rows remain independent. Startup and every completed whole index invoke the same repair. Repository publication markers are removed on startup only after full v2 validation when a v2 root exists; invalid v2 never falls through to legacy marker recovery. Whole-reader generations retain at most the existing two concurrent fills, and a replaced or legacy cache entry closes only after its last active lease releases. Backup's existing derived-search tar now includes each complete focused or v2 repository-search publication byte-for-byte; invalid/incomplete generations are omitted with counts, restore revalidates the complete closed inventory before installation, and precious catalog/state rows therefore restore exactly while an omitted derived generation remains visibly unavailable until rebuilt. Repository removal continues to own all hashed source/search/whole artifacts.** Warm service queries add bounded catalog/state point reads, at most two admitted catalog decodes for genuinely stale state, control-file reads, at most 128 predicate atoms, one exact static reader lease, and final point/control fences; they do not reread source members or hash shards. An exact startup/index no-op performs current repository/catalog/summary reads plus bounded v2 control validation and no Git/blob/member/shard scan, child, write, or activation. Cache fill and a real activation each perform one complete source-member and shard validation; the activation projection decodes once and updates at most 4,000 service rows atomically rather than repeating full-catalog work per service. Backup streams and hashes each selected derived byte once and self-verifies the tar once; explicit verification/restore rereads it and revalidates complete publications. No public HTTP/MCP/UI service-search surface, cursor contract, relationship/evidence claim, target SLO/scale, accuracy/completeness, migration-completion, decommission-safety, or release authority is added; `GATE2-V2` remains `NOT_ESTABLISHED`. | T34.4 owns the shared All code/service product surface and neutral end-to-end demo. | 2026-08-05 | T35.1 generation-scoped chunk scheduler | **High-cardinality future work now has one reusable durable scheduler rather than multiplying the repository job queue by service.** An immutable schedule binds repository, closed stage, semantic generation, CPU/IO/memory resource class, total items, chunk size, attempt limit, and repository-token limit into a domain-separated digest. One current pointer per repository/stage coalesces a newer generation in O(1): prior pending chunks become unreachable without enumeration, running workers retain only their old lease and fail the current-pointer fence, and settled siblings/history remain immutable. A superseded A cannot be resurrected after A→B. Fan-out advances one CAS-fenced page of at most 64 chunks; a logical chunk covers at most 4,096 item ordinals and carries offset/length rather than service identities. Retry creates a fresh immutable attempt identity; cancellation/restart returns the same unconsumed attempt with a cleared lease and deterministic stale priority. Never-run, retry, and stale priorities are respectively 0/1/2 with offset/attempt tie-breaks. Claims consider at most 512 schedule rows, a consequence of the admitted eight active stages per repository and 64 repository candidates, then atomically consume one of at most eight repository tokens. Heartbeats, completion, retry, stale reaping, and coalescing all compare exact physical row, worker, lease, schedule, and current generation. Attempt exhaustion settles only that logical chunk and preserves successful siblings.** The reusable process runner admits only declared class pools whose summed workers, memory, and descriptors fit explicit ceilings: at most 64 workers, 8 GiB declared memory, and 4,096 descriptors process-wide; one chunk may declare at most 1 GiB and 256 descriptors. These are refusal bounds, not measured capacity. Each planner tick selects one schedule and creates at most 64 rows; each reaper selects at most 64 stale rows; one claim reads at most 512 schedule candidates and performs bounded point/CAS attempts; one worker holds one chunk, its declared budget, one heartbeat goroutine, and no corpus/service list. Exact no-op enqueue reads point rows and writes no chunk. No T36/T37 workload is registered yet, so startup, sync, search, evidence, HTTP, MCP, and UI steady state are unchanged apart from idempotent schema definitions. Terminal-row/artifact age/count/byte retention, pins, disk watermarks, and collection remain T35.2–T35.3. | T35.2 owns the pin-aware lifecycle and retention decision. Increasing a bound, adding a resource class, weakening generation/lease fences, resurrecting superseded schedules, or registering a workload requires a new reviewed decision. |
| 2026-08-05 | T35.2 pin-aware lifecycle and retention decision | **Bounded lifecycle becomes an owner-separated, root-first policy; this decision authorizes no deletion until T35.3.** A live repository/catalog/service pointer, current source/search root, or current scheduler pointer is a primary root. A stale service's exact active catalog/source/search identities remain live roots. Durable proof and Investigation ownership is transitive and outranks every age, count, byte, or pressure limit; an active reader or worker lease outranks collection until release or fenced expiry. Backups remain external snapshots, not live pins. After roots and leases, the default rollback floor retains current plus one prior complete generation per repository/owner; only the excess is eligible, oldest-unrooted first. Catalog generations use 30 days, three complete generations, and 64 MiB canonical JSON per repository. Source generations use 14 days, two complete generations, and 8 GiB encoded member bytes per repository; search generations independently use 14 days, two complete generations, and 50 GiB filesystem allocated bytes per repository. Observation, resolver, and relationship namespaces each use 14 days and two complete generations, with independent 20 GiB, 10 GiB, and 20 GiB encoded-member limits per repository. A generation becomes eligible when unrooted and beyond any enabled age, count, or matching byte limit, but collection may not cross the rollback floor. Partial/unpublished stages use a 24-hour grace and at most two abandoned stages per repository/stage; their allocated bytes charge the owning artifact class. Settled generation schedules/chunks use seven days and two settled schedules per repository/stage, while the existing eight durable job tables retain 30 days and at most 100,000 terminal rows per table. Row counts are not bytes. Current service tombstones are precious, indefinite ABA/incarnation fences and have no automatic age/count/byte release; a removed service must durably publish its tombstone before its prior generation loses the live-root set. Proof bundles and Investigation/Workbench records remain indefinite by default and release only through their existing explicit owner lifecycle; proof-bundle TTL continues to remove only that bundle and its exact pins. Reader cache entries have no time/count policy: retirement occurs immediately after replacement and final lease release.** Automated lifecycle is enabled by default once T35.3 installs it. `lifecycle.enabled: false` disables age/count/byte and pressure collection but never weakens roots, leases, tombstones, or existing owner-specific proof-bundle TTL; at the hard watermark it still refuses new derived-artifact and partial-stage admission rather than deleting. Disk pressure is measured on the filesystem containing `server.data_dir` using allocated bytes: 80% starts accelerated bounded collection, 90% refuses new derived work, and admission resumes only below 75%. Unknown capacity reports unavailable and refuses only pressure-dependent new T35 workloads. Pressure never evicts a root, pin, lease, rollback-floor generation, tombstone, proof, or Investigation. Canonical JSON bytes, encoded member bytes, filesystem logical bytes, filesystem allocated bytes, and database row counts are separate typed metrics and may never be summed or substituted; an unavailable metric disables only its matching byte rule and remains visible. Existing T30.6m unbounded history remains physically unchanged until T35.3 migrates each named owner, and unsupported owners remain unbounded rather than being swept by analogy. T35.3 must implement restart-resumable bounded discovery, root rechecks immediately before deletion, fair owner progress, pressure admission, backup coordination, and explicit exact/lower-bound/unavailable status without startup full-history work.** | T35.3 owns implementation and may reduce destructive scope, but it may not increase a limit, invent an owner, combine byte kinds, collect a protected identity, or make disabled lifecycle delete data without a new retained decision. T35.4 owns the recovery/pressure demo. |

| 2026-08-06 | T39.R1 mirror-lock contention closure | **The T39.2 terminal incremental pipeline failure is reproduced and classified as a caller-leaf budget-boundary defect, not evidence that the immutable-mirror lock, caller policy, or retry count is too small.** Aggregate extraction and caller-leaf execution correctly share one repository lock, but caller-leaf previously started its five-minute execution context before waiting for that lock. An admitted extraction could therefore consume the caller budget while the caller performed no work; the resulting ordinary retry could repeat until the unchanged three-attempt runner limit failed the job. Caller pointer/control preflight now retains its own at-most-five-minute context, mirror acquisition uses the lease-heartbeated parent job context, and a fresh at-most-five-minute caller execution context starts only after lock acquisition. The lock, admission, retry accounting, five-minute production limit, three-attempt limit, policy digests, and successor-first publication/repair fences are unchanged. Parent cancellation or lease loss still interrupts the wait before caller-plan open or caller-state mutation; a genuine post-acquisition deadline remains retryable; prior publication remains authoritative until existing repair fences complete.** Warm current callers still return during pointer/control preflight without taking the lock. A noncurrent turn adds no query, read, hash, allocation, or child while queued; it retains one runner lease and one process lock waiter, with heartbeat work unchanged. After acquisition it performs the same bounded caller work as before. Total claim wall time may now be bounded preflight plus external lock occupancy plus bounded execution rather than one five-minute envelope, but lock occupancy remains serialized per repository and shutdown/lease cancellation remains prompt. The deterministic source-free receipt is `spike/t39r1/results.json` (`sha256:92a2751a9d6f31f5e3c9d692bb9fd2022ac84d9bdf9e143f76504cab5c1e502e`); it binds the exact stopped T39.2 and no-release T39.5 receipts and changes no release decision. | T39.2 remains stopped and nonsuperseded. | 2026-08-06 | Design charter and Epic 43 product-experience program | **Adopt `docs/DESIGN_CHARTER.md` as the presentation authority and schedule the parallel presentation-only Epic 43, with T43.1 as its first ticket.** Half of adoption risk is experience: provenance, explicit gaps, staleness, and refusals must feel deliberate rather than apologetic, or the product's honesty reads as clutter. The charter fixes three things and this decision makes them binding for every product surface. Philosophy: calm honesty — the answer leads, its authority is one gesture away, the full audit one further; digest strings never lead; nothing is implied that authority does not establish. Discipline: every rendered datum maps to a named receipt or contract field checked at concept time; malformed or crossed-authority responses render fail-closed; all loading/empty/sparse/filtered/partial/stale/error/retry/unavailable states are designed, textual, and announced; view state lives in the URL; every UI ticket ends demoable on the neutral cohorts with dual-viewport QA, keyboard-complete journeys, a clean console, an accessibility pass, environment-bound deterministic screenshot receipts, suite growth, and recorded bundle/latency notes; walkthrough measurements on neutral cohorts are mechanics evidence only. Design language: one closed five-state status palette paired with state words, monospace identities and tabular counts, "as of" staleness chips, chip → drawer → receipt disclosure, collapsing-never-disappearing caveats with contract-exact wording, citations as objects, table-primary evidence with subordinate diagrams, two densities, keyboard-first navigation, state-transition-only motion on duration/easing tokens with a reduced-motion mapping, honest product voice, and a WCAG AA floor. Epic 43 applies this in twelve dependency-ordered tickets: audit ledger, semantic tokens, shared evidence kit, screenshot receipts, authority drawer, caveat/staleness presentation, citation objects, scope context bar, keyboard navigator, operator console cards, density at ten-thousand rows, and a motion/closure pass. | The charter and Epic 43 govern presentation only: no store, schema, pipeline, endpoint, authorization, claim, caveat-wording, or scale-plane change, and no new reads beyond existing authorized surfaces. This decision supersedes only the single-next-ticket clause of the post-Epic-39 ADR by adding one parallel presentation track; T40.1 is complete, T40.2 is the next scale ticket, and Epics 40-42 sequencing is unchanged. Screenshot receipts and walkthrough timings establish presentation mechanics, never usability validation, accuracy, supported scale, or release; all packs remain experimental-dark, `GATE2-V2` remains `NOT_ESTABLISHED`, and `DO_NOT_RELEASE` remains in force. |

T40's smaller semantic profile independently carries at least 262,144 distinct eligible Go blobs and at least 32,768 IDL candidates, with closed supported and explicit-gap families. T41 separately authors accepted-only 8,000-, 10,000-, and 12,500-service catalogs plus a small transition profile; accepted authority is explicit rather than inferred from directory/import shape, and shared placements are partitioned into groups whose accepted fan-out remains at most 20. T42 composes the exact 10,000-service authority over the exact two-million-eligible-Go-path structural shape plus separately reported controls without materializing service-count × file-count bytes. The author streams into an external scratch bare Git repository with fixed identity, timestamps, modes, path order, profile version, and seeds; emits baseline A, bounded B, and A-content-return revisions; and retains only source-free schemas, generators, independent oracles, manifests, identities, and receipts in this repository. Bulk generated-code shape comes from closed deterministic templates; a small pinned real-generator parity subset may validate templates, but `protoc`, Buf, sqlc, ent, Bazel, and Gazelle are not bulk authoring dependencies. Public runtime traces may inform neutral aggregate DAG families only after license review; their rows and identities are neither vendored nor treated as source, catalog, membership, relationship, or expected-result authority. | T40.1 must freeze each profile's placements, unique blobs, declared/logical/encoded/allocated bytes, path layout, transition deltas, failure points, generator/tool identities, and expected pass/refusal outcomes before a production aggregate changes. Normal tests retain small deterministic projections; giant repositories are explicit authoring/gate artifacts outside the worktree. Git pack bytes and third-party generator bytes are toolchain-sensitive measurements, not durable semantic identities. The one-root-module choice is a fixture simplification, not an unmeasured claim that multi-module tools crash. |
